Executive summary of study results: O see Attached
The ability of several anti-lysophosphatidic acid (anti-LPA) antibodies (both murine, 63, and humanized: LT 3015;
LT 3017; LT 3114) to bind to a range of lipids that are structurally and chemically similar to LP was examined in a
competition ELISA format. Lipids at varying concentrations were added to the test antibodies in a 3:1 ratio (Lipid:Ab) and
then added to plates (100 IJI/well in triplicate) coated with 18:0 LPA and the ability of the antibodies to bind to the plate in
the presence of potentially competing ligands was assessed.
There was no significant cross-reactivity of any of the antibodies with the lipids tested up to a concentration of 5 iJM lipid
with the exception of LPA itself.

<!-- Page 4 -->

Report Number: LT3114-PHA-014-R
Title: Comparison of the binding of anti-LPA antibodies to other Page 3 of 20
lipid molecules (cross-reactivity)
1 SUMMARY
The ability of several anti-lysophosphatidic acid (anti-LPA) antibodies (both murine, B3, and
humanized: LT3015; LT3017; LT3114) to bind to a range of lipids that are structurally and
chemically similar to LPA was examined in a competition ELISA format. Lipids at varying
concentrations were added to the test antibodies in a 3:1 ratio (Lipid:Ab) and then added to
plates (100 µl/well in triplicate) coated with 18:0 LPA and the ability of the antibodies to
bind to the plate in the presence of potentially competing ligands was assessed.
There was no significant cross-reactivity of any of the antibodies with the lipids tested up to a
concentration of 5 µM lipid with the exception of LPA itself.
2 INTRODUCTION
The bioactive lipid lysophosphatidic acid (LPA) is an important extracellular signaling
molecule in neuropathic pain. LPA and its receptors are responsible for long-lasting
mechanical allodynia and thermal hyperalgesia as well as demyelination and upregulation of
pain-related protein expression patterns in a variety of animal models. Evidence supports the
hypothesis that antagonism of the LPA signaling pathways would benefit the treatment of
neuropathic pain. We have developed the first high affinity monoclonal antibodies targeting
LPA (anti-LPA mAb) and the studies described here were performed to examine the
potential for these antibodies to bind other lipids which are structurally similar to LPA.
3 MATERIALS AND METHODS
3.1 Test Articles
The range of lipids tested in this study is shown in Table 1. Antibodies that were used to examine
competitive binding are detailed in Table 2.

3.2 ELISA methodology
The specificity of the humanized antibody was determined by competition ELISA. 18:0 LPA
coating material is diluted to 0.33 µg/ml in carbonate buffer (100 mM NaHCO , 33.6 mM
3
Na CO pH 9.5). Plates are coated with 100 µl/well of this coating solution and incubated at
2 3,
37°C for 1 hour. The plates are then washed 4X with PBS (100 mM Na HPO 20 mM
2 4,
KH PO 27 mM KCl, 1.37 mM NaCl, pH 7.4) and blocked with 150 µl/well PBS + 1% BSA
2 4,
+ 0.1% tween-20 for 1 hour at room temperature. The humanized anti-LPA antibody
variants and the murine B3 antibody were tested against lipids that are structurally and
chemically similar to LPA at 5 µM, 2.5 µM, 1.25 µM, 0.625 µM, and 0.0 µuM. The
antibodies are diluted to 0.5 µg/ml in PBS +0.1 % tween-20 and combined with the lipid
samples at a 1:3 ratio of antibody to sample on a non-binding plate (recommended 100 µl Ab

Report Number: LT3114-PHA-014-R
Title: Comparison of the binding of anti-LPA antibodies to other Page 6 of 20
lipid molecules (cross-reactivity)
and 300 µl samples/standards, enough to run in triplicate). The plates are washed 4X with
PBS and then incubated for 1 hour at room temperature with 100 µl/well of the primary
antibody combined with the samples. Next the plates are washed 4X with PBS and then
incubated for 1 hour at room temperature with 100 µl/well of HRP-conjugated goat anti-
human or murine antibody diluted 1:20,000 in PBS + 1% BSA + 0.1% tween-20. Again the
plates are washed 4X with PBS and developed using 100 µl/well TMB substrate at
4°C. After 8 minutes, the reaction is stopped with 1M H SO 100 µl/well. The optical
2 4,
density (OD) was measured at 450 nm using a Thermo Multiskan EX. Raw data were
transferred to GraphPad software for analysis of competition curves. Values for percent
inhibition at 2.5 µM lipid were also calculated for each lipid using Microsoft Excel.
4 RESULTS
Competition curves for all lipids tested are shown. Individual data tables for these lipids are
detailed in Appendix 1. Data showing the percent inhibition of all lipids at 2.5uM is
summarized in Table 3. Overall, as expected the only lipid that demonstrated significant
competition with the coating material (18:0 LPA) for binding was LPA 18:2. 18:1 alkyl-LPA
also showed a low level of inhibition in these assay but none of the remaining lipids showed
a significant level of cross-reactivity as measured by competition for plated 18:0 LPA.
CONFIDENTIAL
